<image-desc>The Google logo in its characteristic multi-colored (blue, red, yellow, blue, green, red) serif font, with a small superscript trademark symbol.</image-desc>

This is a digital copy of a book that was preserved for generations on library shelves before it was carefully scanned by Google as part of a project to make the world’s books discoverable online.

It has survived long enough for the copyright to expire and the book to enter the public domain. A public domain book is one that was never subject to copyright or whose legal copyright term has expired. Whether a book is in the public domain may vary country to country. Public domain books are our gateways to the past, representing a wealth of history, culture and knowledge that’s often difficult to discover.

Marks, notations and other marginalia present in the original volume will appear in this file - a reminder of this book’s long journey from the publisher to a library and finally to you.

## Usage guidelines

Google is proud to partner with libraries to digitize public domain materials and make them widely accessible. Public domain books belong to the public and we are merely their custodians. Nevertheless, this work is expensive, so in order to keep providing this resource, we have taken steps to prevent abuse by commercial parties, including placing technical restrictions on automated querying.

We also ask that you:

+ *Make non-commercial use of the files* We designed Google Book Search for use by individuals, and we request that you use these files for personal, non-commercial purposes.

+ *Refrain from automated querying* Do not send automated queries of any sort to Google’s system: If you are conducting research on machine translation, optical character recognition or other areas where access to a large amount of text is helpful, please contact us. We encourage the use of public domain materials for these purposes and may be able to help.

+ *Maintain attribution* The Google “watermark” you see on each file is essential for informing people about this project and helping them find additional materials through Google Book Search. Please do not remove it.

+ *Keep it legal* Whatever your use, remember that you are responsible for ensuring that what you are doing is legal. Do not assume that just because we believe a book is in the public domain for users in the United States, that the work is also in the public domain for users in other countries. Whether a book is still in copyright varies from country to country, and we can’t offer guidance on whether any specific use of any specific book is allowed. Please do not assume that a book’s appearance in Google Book Search means it can be used in any manner anywhere in the world. Copyright infringement liability can be quite severe.

## About Google Book Search

Google’s mission is to organize the world’s information and to make it universally accessible and useful. Google Book Search helps readers discover the world’s books while helping authors and publishers reach new audiences. You can search through the full text of this book on the web at <insert>http://books.google.com/</insert>

At the tips of the main stem and some branches are small, rounded clusters of flowers or seeds. The plant emerges from a thick, curved root system. The entire illustration is enclosed within a simple double-line rectangular frame.</image-desc>

### ¶ De absinthio .

<image-desc>A woodcut illustration contained within a rectangular double-border. It depicts a wormwood plant (Absinthium) with a central stem from which several large, deeply-lobed, and serrated leaves branch out. The leaves have a feathery, pointed appearance characteristic of the Artemisia genus. The base of the stem curves slightly to the right, showing the starting point of the root system.</image-desc>

¶ In primo calor et c. Absinthij herbe quida[m] vul-
garissime sed nihilominus excellentissime deter-

### De ruta herba. vij.

Sicceque fertur in his gradus illi tertius esse

Utilis est valde stomacho si sepe bibatur

Expellit partum potu veneremque cohercet.
Tussim si bibitur compescit: menstrua purgat

Compescit talis decoctio tormina ventris

**A medicis ru. etc Rute vires atque proprietates**

Plinius lib. xx. capitulo decimo tertio illustrissime perstringit cuius initium est . In precipuis autem medicaminibus ruta est quam Macer exactissime imitatur vis: id est qualitas fervida: id est calida et sicca dicitur esse a medicis ruta: id est in ruta / et tertius gradus fertur esse in his scilicet in caliditate et siccitate hoc est calida et sicca in tertio gradu: ruta est utilis: id est accommoda stomacho debili et frigido si bibatur sepe ipsa expellit id est educit partum id est fetus seu embryponem potu si bibatur succus eius. et cohercet. id est cohibet venerem : id est appetitum ad venerea si bibitur compescit : id est sedat tussim purgat: id est provocat menstrua.

Si coquis : id est decoquis hanc herbam in aqua cui iunxeris: id est coniunxeris vinum acre: id est acetum talis decoctio id est liquor talis decoctionis compescit: id est pacatur tormina: id est tortiones ventris: id est intestinorum Tormen genus est morbi cum torquentur intestina a torquendo deductum. Plinius. Mentastrum torminibus cole-

¶ Bracteos. &c. De sauina eiusque proprietatibus
paucis absoluit. hanc Plinius lib. 24. ca. 11. sabi-

### Lingua bouis herba.xxxiiii.

Lingua bouis greco sermone buglossa vocatur
Hec coleram rubeam nimio feruore perustam
Purgat: cum vino fuerit si sumpta frequenter
Cardiacam colere quam fecit copia nigre
Cum simili potata modo curare valebit.
Humores nocuos pulmonis detrahit hausta
Mixtus aque tepide si succus sumitur eius
Illi qui patitur sciasim mire medicatur
Vim memorem cerebri dicunt seruare periti.

<vocab>Lingua bouis, buglossa, coleram rubeam, cardiacam, humores, sciasim, cerebri, herba</vocab>

--- Page 166 ---
<language>Latin</language>
<page-type>text</page-type>
<meta>This page contains a commentary on medical verses, likely from an early printed edition of the Regimen Sanitatis Salernitanum. The text discusses the medicinal properties of the herb Buglossa (Bugloss), citing authorities such as Marsilio Ficino, Avicenna, and Macer Floridus. The typeface is a Gothic Blackletter.</meta>

> Vinum potatum quo sit macerata buglossa
> Letos conuiuas decoctio dicitur eius
> Reddere si fuerit inter conuiuia sparsa

¶ Hec herba a grecis euphralmum id est herba le-
tificans nuncupatur. greci enim euphraim letari di-
cunt : nam colere nigre que ( vt inquit Marcillius fic.)
ipsa morte deterior est capitalis est inimica. Cupien-
tes igitur dies agere letos huius vires leto percipia-
mus animo lingua bouis vocatur buglossa sermone
greco. hec herba purgat id est exturbat colerā rube-
am perustam id est adustam nimio feruore si frequē-
ter fuerit sumpta cum vino nam vt inquit princeps
Auicenna : colere nigre id est melencolie : aliis est na-
turalis : alia non naturalis. Naturalis est fex seu
hyppostatis aliorum humorum. Non naturalis ve-
ro fit ex incineratione & adustione aliorum humorū

# ¶ De zedoar herba. Lxxi.

# ¶ De cinnama herba.

<image-desc>A large decorative woodcut initial 'C' containing a bird (likely a swan or goose) with its neck curved back, nestled within a square frame featuring foliate decorative elements.</image-desc>

Cinnama tres species dicuntur habere
sed harum.
Est p̄ciosa magis q̄ pl⁹ subtilis habet̄
Et q̄ pl⁹ mordet mixta dulcediē liguās
Humores stomachi siccat: corrobozat ipsū
Et facit acceptas vt digeret protinus escas

ydropicas speciem (cui p̄bent timpana nomen)

Que curant oculos: humores siccat aquosos:
Si bene contritum forti miscetur aceto.
Liberat appositum tetra lentigine vultum.
Sicqȝ iuuat mozbum qui ducit ab impete nomen
Grossa magis species fluxus emoroydarum

Illius geminas cum mulsa sumere dragmas
Idem præcepit: coleram sic flegmaque purgat:
Unaquaque die post cenam sumere dixit:
Utile tres eius pilullas binas ve recentes
Cum succo caulis formatas ut faba grossas.
Aut cicer humores nocuos cum stercore purgat
Non corrupendo stomachum neque vim faciendo
Purgatus fuerit pilullis qui sepius istis
Vix unquam capitis vexabitur ille dolore.
Et nil est oculis mihi crede salubrius istis.
Si vero curam vis solvere largius alvum
Partibus est geminis pars admiscenda terendo
Una diagridii sic apta solutio fiet.
